.

See that the output is typed via subtyping.
Then, we have:

{\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}_{\mathrm{Auth}}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}})}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}_{\mathrm{Auth}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{send}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\#\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}\#{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{auth}}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\text{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_string}{\color[rgb]{0.4,0,0}{"passwd"}}}}}}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{auth}}}}{({\mbox{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 6.99997pt}}}{,}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}}})}}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{recv}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x^{\prime}}\#\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}}}}}\end{array}

Theorem C.8

(Subject reduction)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}}}
.

Appendix D
Proofs for Basic Properties of
MiO

D.1
Substitution Lemma and other lemmas

Lemma D.1

(Substitution lemma)
.

Followings hold:

1.

(a) If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
(b) Moreover, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{0}}}]}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{0}}}]}
.

2.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}]}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}]}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.

3.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}}
.

Proof.

Follows.

1.

We proceed by mutual induction on the derivation trees of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}]}
.
We start from (a).

Case

[
Otc
-
()
]
.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\texttt{()}}
. Trivial.

Case

[
Otc
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
]
.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
,
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}
,
and by rule
[
Otc
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
]
,
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
.
By assumption, we get
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
, it trivially holds.

Case

[
Otc
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
]
.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
.
We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
and it trivially holds.

Case

[
Otc-Tup
]
.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}},\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}})
.
For all
i\in\{1,\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},n\}
, exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
such that
T={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}},\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}})}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}{\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}
,
and we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}
for
i\in\{1,\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},n\}
.
By induction hypothesis, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\quad(i\in\{1,\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},n\})
.
Then, by applying
[
Otc-Tup
]
, we get

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}},\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}})\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}{\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp}}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}}
.

Case

[
Otc-Record
]
and
[
Otc-Variant
]
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}l_{i}}}{=}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\right\rangle_{i\in I}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}l}}{=}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\right]}{}
. Similar.

Case

[
Otc-WrapInp
]
.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}{{\mathtt{@}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}}\right]_{i\in I}}
.
From rule
[
Otc-WrapInp
]
,
T={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}?}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}
,
and for each
i\in I
, there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}]}
.
By induction hypothesis, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}]}
for each
i\in I
, and
by applying
[
Otc-WrapInp
]
, it follows
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}{{\mathtt{@}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}}\right]_{i\in I}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}?}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}}}
.

Case

[
Otc-Sub
]
.
We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}}
.
By induction hypothesis,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}}
.
Again, by applying
[
Otc-Sub
]
, we get
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.

For
(b), we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{0}}}]}
and the only rule is
[
Otc-Wrapper
]
.
By the rule, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\notin\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\right)
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}]
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{0}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
Note that, by Barendregt convention, we can assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\notin\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\right)
.
By induction hypothesis,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{0}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
,
Furthermore, we see
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}]

and,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\notin\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\right)
(since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\notin\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\right)
).
By
[
Otc-Wrapper
]
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{0}}}]}
.

2.

From the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}]}
, for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}]
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
By (1), we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}

and since
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}]={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}
,
we get
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}]}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.

Lemma D.2

(Inversion)
.

Followings hold:

1.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{send}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}{,}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{j}}}\right)}

then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{j}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}

where
j\in I
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
.

2.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{recv}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}{{\mathtt{@}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}}\right]_{i\in I}}

then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}]}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
for all
i\in I
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}
.

3.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{match}\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{with}\mkern 2.5mu\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}{i}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y_{i}}})\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{\mathnormal{j}}}}{=}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{j}}}{,}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{j}}}\right)}}\right]}{}

,
then
for all

,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{j}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{j}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{j}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}
.

4.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{letrec}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}}({\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}})={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}}}\;\mathbf{in}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{2}}}}}}

, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}X}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}}
,

\forall i\in 1..n
,

and

7.

,
then

\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{1}}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{n}}}\}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}}

8.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathbf{\nu}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}){{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}}}

then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}

Lemma D.3

(Type preservation for
\equiv
)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}}}
.

Proof.

Standard.
∎

The following lemma relates term-level and type-level projection.

D.2
Type safety for global combinators

Definition D.4
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
is
basic on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
,
written
\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}})
,
if, for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\in\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)
there is some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}}
for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}\in\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)
s.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}
, .

Lemma D.5
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}

(i\in\{1,2\})
and followings hold:

1.

\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.

2.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}{\mapsto}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}})}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}

(i\in\{1,2\})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}
is defined and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
holds.

Proof.

We proceed by the induction on the number of calls of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}
.
This induction terminates since the size of the set of pairs
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}})
accumulated in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}
is bounded.
The interesting cases are ones that involve recursion.

Case

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}}
.
(1) If
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}{\mapsto}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}})}\in\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}
,
by the definition, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}
.
Furthermore, by assumption, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
(2) If
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}{\mapsto}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}})}\notin\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}
,
by inversion lemma, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}{.}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}}

for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}{.}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
and by substitution lemma,
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}{.}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}}
.
Furthermore, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}{.}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}{.}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
By induction hypothesis,
we have some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}{\mapsto}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}})}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}

defined, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.
(Here, beware that both

\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}})

and

\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}})

hold since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}
is fresh, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}\notin\left(\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{1}}}\right)\cup\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}}\right)\right)
).
Then, by
[
Otc
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu}
]
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z}}{\mathbin{.}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.

Case

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
.
Since
\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}})
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}
, and
by inversion lemma,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}
.
Furthermore, since only possible rule to derive
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}
is

[
Otc
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
]
, and from
\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}})
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
.
Hence, by the definition of
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}}}
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sqcup}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\chi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{2}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
and
\mathrm{Basic}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}})
for all
i\in I
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bigsqcup}_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}}
is defined
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bigsqcup}_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
.

Proof.

Straightforward by induction.
∎

Proposition D.7
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
is closed.

By induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}
.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{1}}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{n}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}

then
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}
is defined and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}

where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}\}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}\in\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}

for some
\{\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}\}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}
.

Case

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\texttt{(}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{j}}}}\,\rightarrow\,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{k}}}}\texttt{)}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}}
.
By inversion,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}
holds.
By induction hypothesis, we get

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}\}
for some

\{\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}\}
.
Let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{j}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{k}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{i}}\}}

For each

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({i})}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}

and see that by
[
Otc
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}

Then,
by applying typing rules repeatedly, we have:

and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\texttt{choice}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{a}}}}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}_{i}}}\right\}_{i\in I}}

i\in I
we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Bigl\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{a}}}}{:}\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{\mathnormal{k}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{!{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}}}{\times}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}}}}\rangle_{k\in K_{i}}\Bigr\rangle}\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\scriptstyle\times}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}}

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{\widetilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{ij}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{ij}}}\}

and

for each

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{ia}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{a}}}}{=}{\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{\mathnormal{k}}}}{=}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{ik}}}{,}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{ik}}}\right)}}}\right\rangle_{k\in K_{i}}}\right\rangle}

D.3
Proof of Subject Reduction

See
C.8

Proof.

Induction on derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}
.

Case

[
Ored-Comm
]
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{send}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\#{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}\#{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{\mathnormal{k}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{recv}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\#{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{2}}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{2}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{j}}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}]}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}}}\right\}}}

where
j\in I
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\#{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}\#{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{\mathnormal{k}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}{,}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\#{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}{{\mathtt{@}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}}\right]_{i\in I}}
.
By applying inversion lemma for
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}
,
send
and
recv
, we have

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{send}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d_{1}}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}}}}}
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu\textbf{recv}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d_{2}}\mkern 2.5mu\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{2}}}}}}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}}}
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}

•

For all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime\prime}}}[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}]}

.

By applying substitution lemma on

, we get
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x}}}}\right\}}}}}
.

Otc-Sub
]
to

[
Ored-Match
]
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{match}\mkern 2.5muc\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{with}\mkern 2.5mu\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{\mathnormal{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y_{i}}})\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}}\}_{i\in I}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c^{\prime}_{j}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}

for all

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}}

[

]
.

\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}

•

\forall i\in 1..n
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}}

[
Otc-Sub

Case

[
Ored-Init
]
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{let}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{n}}}\mkern 2.5mu{=}\mkern 2.5mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}\mkern 2.5mu\mathbf{in}\mkern 2.5mu{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{n}})}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathbf{\nu}{\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}){({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}}}}}\right\}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\cdots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{n}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{n}}}}}\right\}}})}}
.
From the premise of the rule, we have:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rrbracket}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{1}}},\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.63885pt\ldotp\kern-1.63885pt\ldotp},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}_{{n}}}\}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}},\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp\kern-1.99997pt\ldotp},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}}}\right)}
,

•

\bigcup{}_{i\in\{1\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.63885pt\ldotp\kern-1.63885pt\ldotp}n\}}\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\right)=\{\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\}
, which shares base name
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
and

•

\{\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}\}\cap\bigcup{}_{i\in\{1\mathinner{\ldotp\kern-1.63885pt\ldotp\kern-1.63885pt\ldotp}n\}}\operatorname{fn}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}\right)=\emptyset
.

By inversion, we have

•

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{g}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathbb{R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({i})}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
and

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}}
.

From
\Cref
col:SubjectReductionForGC, for

\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}\}_{j\in J}=\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}

we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}}\}_{j\in J}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
By weakening, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we have

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}}\}_{j\in J}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}\mathbin{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}
and

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}}\}_{j\in J}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}}}
.

By substitution lemma, we get

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}\{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s_{j}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sharp}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}}\}_{j\in J}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{i}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{i}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{i}}}}}\right\}}}}}
.
By applying
[
Ot
-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}
]
and
[
Ot
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{\nu}}
]
, we finally get

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\mathbf{\nu}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}){\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{1}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{1}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}}}}}\right\}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\ldots\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{n}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c_{n}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{n}}}}}\right\}}}\right)}}}}
.
∎

Appendix E
Implementation: Omitted Type Signatures and Explanations

This section gives the OCaml type signatures and implementations of
the main implementation building blocks using
sophisticated functional programming techniques based on GADT and polymorphic variants.
Namely, first-class methods and labels are explained in §
E.1
,
roles and variable-length tuples in § sec:vartup,
input and output channels in §
E.3
,
and global combinators in §
E.4
.

Implementation of first-class methods and labels

As we show in §
4.1
, the definition of roles and labels use
methods
of an object.
To enable this encoding, we introduce
first-class
methods –
the type
method_
defined on Line
2
in Fig.
26
.
The type is a record with a
constructor function

make_obj
and
a
destructor function

call_obj
.
An example usage of the type
method_
is given on Line
6
by defining the type
login_method
.
In
make_obj
, the expression
(
object

method

login
=
v

end
)

creates an object that consists of a method
login
with no parameter, returning
(
v
:

’
mt
)
.
Field
call_obj
simply implements a method invocation (
obj
#
login
).

Our encoding of local types requires label names to be encoded as an object method (in case of internal choice) and
as a variant tag (in case of external choice).
Hence, the
label
type, Line
9
, is defined as a pair of a first-class method and
a
variant constructor function
.
As in §
4.2
,
while object and variant constructor functions are needed to compose a channel vector in
(
-->
)
,
object destructor functions are used in
merge
in
choice_at
,
to extract bare channels inside an object.
Variant destructors are not needed, as they are destructed via pattern-matching and merging is
done by
Event
.
choose
of Concurrent ML.
Using the types
method_
,

label
the user can define arbitrary labels.

Then, taking inspiration from

),

The index type

making an index in a tuple via Peano numbers.
The constructor

says that the lens refers to the 0-th element i.e. the head of a cons, while

takes a lens and constructs a new lens which refers to a position deeper by one. By applying

repeatedly, elements at arbitrary depths can be referred. We store the lens for each channel vector inside the role object. For example, the roles

E.3
Input and output types

To represent communication channels,
we use the OCaml module Event, which provides a synchronous inter-thread
communications over channels. For each communication action we generate a fresh channel
and wrap it in a channel vector structure. The output
<
m
:

(
v
*
t
)

out
>
is an object with a method
m
proactively called by the sender’s side choosing label
m
,
of which return type

(
v
*
t
)

out
is just a pair of channel and continuation.

type

(
’
v
,

’
t
)

out

=

’
v

Event
.
channel

*

’
t

(
*

abstract

*)

where
’
v

Event
.
channel
is a standard synchronous channel type of value
’
v
in OCaml.
Note that this pair structure is abstract i.e., hidden outside the module,
to prevent abusing of the continuation
’
t
before sending on
’
v

channel
.
The output on
’
v

channel
does not transmit any labels, but they are
implicitly
passed.
The transmission of the label
m

implicitly
happens,
when output labels are proactively chosen by calling a method
m
.
The input
[
>
‘
m

of

v
*
t
]

inp
makes an external choice as an idiomatic pattern-matching on variants,
enabling a case analysis on continuations based on labels.
This is done by
Event
.
wrap
function, which originates from Concurrent ML
[
52
]
.
The
wrap
function works as a
map
on received values;
thus, by wrapping
v

channel
with a function
v

-
>

[
>
‘
m

of

v
*
t
]
,
we obtain an input of type
[
>
‘
m

of

v
*
t
]

inp
.

Loops via lazy evaluation

The signature of the loop combinator
fix
is given below.

⬇

val␣fix␣:␣(
’
t␣tup␣->␣
’
t␣tup)␣->␣
’
t␣tup

let␣fix␣f␣
=
␣let␣rec␣body␣
=
␣lazy␣(f␣(RecVar␣body))␣in␣Lazy
.
force␣body

Function
fix
takes a function
f
and returns a fixpoint of it
(
x

=

f

x
)

by utilising lazy evaluation and a
value recursion
which makes a cyclic data structure.
We extend the tuple types for global combinators, i.e
’
t

tup
type, with a new constructor
RecVar
which discriminates recursion variables from other constructors.

Lazy
.
force
tries to expand unguarded recursion variables which occurs right under the fixpoint combinator.
This enables the “fail-fast” policy, explained in §
4.1
. For example, an unguarded loop like
(
fix

(
fun

t

-
>

t
))
fails with
UnguardedLoop
exception.

E.5
Example of Concatenating Two Disjoint Objects

The functions
disj_merge
concatenates two disjoint objects
’

and
’
r
into one, while

\{

,

\}
splits an object
’

to
’
l
and
’
r
, respectively.
Both are used in the definition of a branching operator.
This constraint must manually be supplied by programmers.
For example, the following
left_or_right
states
a concatenation of type
<

:

’

>
and
<

:

’

>
into

’

>

⬇

<

’
l
;
␣right:␣
’
r>,␣
<
left:␣
’
l>,␣
<

’
r>)␣disj

let␣left_or_right␣
=

␣␣
{
disj_merge
=
(
fun␣l␣r␣->␣object␣method␣left
=
l
#
left␣method␣right
=
r
#
right␣end)
;

␣␣␣disj_split_L
=
(
fun␣obj␣->␣obj
#
left)
;
␣disj_split_R
=
(

#
right)
}

Appendix F
Multiparty Session Types and Processes

This section quickly outlines the multiparty session types

[
12
,
56
]
.
For the syntax of types, we follow

[
4
]
which is the most widely used syntax in the
literature.
A
global type
, written
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G},{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime},..
,
describes the whole
conversation scenario of a multiparty session as a type signature, and
a
local type
, written by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}^{\prime},\cdots
.
Let
\!\mathscr{P}\!
be a set of
participants fixed throughout the section:
\!\mathscr{P}\!=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}},\cdots\}
, and
\mathbb{A}
is a set of alphabets.

Definition F.1

(Global types)
.

The syntax of a
global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is:

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,\Coloneqq\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{i}}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})\vphantom{x}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\neq\!{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}
,
I\!\neq\!\emptyset
,  and
\forall i\!\in\!I:\operatorname{fv}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}}\right)=\emptyset

We write
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}
(or simply
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\in\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
)  iff, for some
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}
, either
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}{\to}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}{\to}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
occurs in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
.

Definition F.2

(Local types)
.

The syntax of
local types
is:

\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}T}\,\Coloneqq\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\oplus_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{end}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\ \text{with $I\!\neq\!\emptyset$,
and ${\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}$ pairwise distinct}

We require types to be closed, and recursion variables to be guarded.

The relation between global and local types is formalised by

projection

[
4
,
27
]
.

Definition F.3

(projection)
.

The

projection of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
onto

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}
(written
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
)
is defined as:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\left({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}}_{i}}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})\vphantom{x}{\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\right)}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\;=\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0.5mm}l@{\hskip 5mm}l@{}}1.42262pt\lx@intercol\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\oplus_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}$}\\
1.42262pt\lx@intercol\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}$}\\
1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}$}\end{array}\right.\\[14.22636pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}})}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\;=\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\hskip 0.5mm}l@{\hskip 5mm}l@{}}1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}^{\prime}}$ ($\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}^{\prime}}$)}\\
1.42262pt\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{end}}\hfil\hskip 14.22636pt&\text{\footnotesize otherwise}\end{array}\right.\qquad\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{\hskip 1mm}c@{\hskip 1mm}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\hskip 2.84526pt&=\hfil\hskip 2.84526pt&{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{end}}\end{array}\end{array}

For projection of branchings, we appeal to a merge
operator along the lines of
[
14
]
, written
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}^{\prime}
, ensuring
that if the locally observable behaviour of the local type is
dependent of the chosen branch then it is identifiable via a unique
choice/branching label.
The
merging operation

\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}{}{}
is defined as a partial commutative
operator over two types such that:

\begin{array}[]{c}\textstyle\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{\!j\in J}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{\!j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}T^{\prime}_{j}}}}\;=\;\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{k\in I\cap J}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{k}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{k}}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}T^{\prime}_{k}})}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&}}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{i\in I\setminus J}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&}}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{\!j\in J\setminus I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{\!j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}T^{\prime}_{j}}}}\\[2.84526pt]
\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\oplus_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\oplus_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\;=\;\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\oplus_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S_{i}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S^{\prime}_{i}}}}\\[2.84526pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}T}}}\;=\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}T})}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\;=\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{end}}\,\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\sqcap}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{end}}\;=\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\mathbf{end}}\end{array}

We say that
G
is
well-formed
if for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\!\mathscr{P}\!
,

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is defined.

Below we define the
multiparty session subtyping relation
, following

[
24
]
[
17
]
.
10
10

10

For convenience, we use the “channel-oriented” order of
[
21
,
54
]
for our subtyping relation. For a comparison with
“process-oriented” subtyping of
[
17
]
,
see
[
22
]
.
Intuitively, a type
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}
is smaller than
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}^{\prime}
when
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}
is “less demanding” than
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}^{\prime}
,
i.e., when
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}S}
imposes to support less external choices
and allows to perform more internal choices. Session subtyping is used in the type system to augment its flexibility.

Definition F.4

(Session subtyping)
.

The subtyping relation
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\leqslant}}
is
co
inductively defined as:

\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.4,0.1}\&_{i\in
